[PATCH RFC v2 1/8] vsock: expose the core port allocator to
Author: Sergio Lopez <slp@redhat.com>

The core port allocator is currently private to connection-oriented
sockets. Virtio datagrams need the core bound table and port allocation,
while VMCI datagrams must keep their own endpoint creation and port
namespace.

Export vsock_bind_common() with the bound-table locking inside the
helper, and use it for stream and SEQPACKET binds. Keep datagram binds
delegated to the transport so a transport can opt into the core
allocator without changing other transports' binding behavior.

Keep datagram and connection-oriented sockets in separate logical port
spaces within the core bound table. Filter bind conflicts and bound-socket
lookups by class, including wildcard CIDs and automatic port allocation,
so datagram and stream/SEQPACKET sockets can share a CID and port. Stream
and SEQPACKET sockets continue to conflict with each other.

Signed-off-by: Sergio Lopez <slp@redhat.com>
---

[kvm-unit-tests PATCH] Move position-independant code flags into the target specific Makefiles
Author: Thomas Huth <thuth@redhat.com>

The settings for position-independant code are currently quite a mess:
It depends on the target whether we need the flags or rather should even
disable PIC / PIE, but currently the settings are spread between the
main Makefile (-fno-pic is the default, unless CONFIG_EFI has been set)
and the target specific Makefiles.
This leads to some weird situations where we have -fno-pic in the CFLAGS,
but link with -pie in the LDFLAGS since we still want to get a relocation
table, but then again need to add a "-z notext" to the linker flags to
avoid warnings about relocation entries in the text segment.
Let's face it: Always adding -fno-pic / -no-pie in the main Makefile is
a bad idea. Move the settings to the target specific Makefiles instead
where we know what we really want, and then we can finally drop the
"-z notext" settings, too.

Signed-off-by: Thomas Huth <thuth@redhat.com>
---

 x86/Makefile.common | 5 +++++

[PATCH v4] RISC-V: KVM: Serialize and atomize the IMSIC's TOPEI read-and-clear operation
Author: BillXiang <xiangwencheng@lanxincomputing.com>

The TOPEI read-and-clear must be atomic with respect to
interrupt injection (set_bit in EIP). Otherwise an
interrupt injected between the topei read and the
clear_bit could be silently dropped.

[RFC PATCH 12/20] hw/misc: add the RK3588 OTP controller
Author: Yifan Wang <w-yf22@mails.tsinghua.edu.cn>


Add a model of the RK3588 OTP automatic read interface that U-Boot uses
for CPU identity and device-tree initialization. The OTP array starts
unprogrammed: a read command fills up to four data registers with zero
words and sets a write-one-to-clear completion status bit. Commands
outside the 1024-word array are rejected as guest errors.

[RFC PATCH 11/20] hw/misc: add the RK3588 clock and reset unit
Author: Yifan Wang <w-yf22@mails.tsinghua.edu.cn>


Add a model of the RK3588 CRU registers that U-Boot programs before it
configures the SPI flash controller clock. The model stores the PLL
parameters and clock selections. CON0/CON1 and clock selections use
Rockchip high-word write masks; fractional PLL CON2 stores a 16-bit K

[RFC PATCH 04/20] target/arm: allow machines to handle SMC calls
Author: Yifan Wang <w-yf22@mails.tsinghua.edu.cn>


Some machines model firmware services which use vendor SMC function IDs
while QEMU provides PSCI through the same SMC conduit.  There is
currently no way for such a machine to take over a call before the PSCI
path handles it.

Add a small registry for SMC function ID handlers.  Machines can
register a handler which inspects the call and returns whether it was
handled, without changing the behavior of existing callers.

[RFC PATCH 03/20] target/arm: report SMCCC v1.1 support
Author: Yifan Wang <w-yf22@mails.tsinghua.edu.cn>


Guests use the SMCCC version call to select the conduit for firmware
services such as SCMI.  QEMU reports PSCI 1.1 but rejects the SMCCC
version call, so a guest running with the SMC PSCI conduit never
initializes SMCCC and cannot use SCMI over SMC.

[RFC PATCH 00/20] hw/arm: Add initial RK3588 and ROCK 5B support
Author: Yifan Wang <w-yf22@mails.tsinghua.edu.cn>

Hi,

This series adds initial support for the Rockchip RK3588 SoC and the
Radxa ROCK 5B board. It supports booting Linux through U-Boot SPL and
U-Boot from SPI NOR flash, as well as direct kernel boot.

The implementation includes the eight Cortex-A55/A76 CPUs, GICv3,
architectural timers, SRAM, UARTs, and the Rockchip SPI flash controller.
It also models the CRU, OTP, and HPTIMER registers needed by the boot
software.

Patches 1-5 extend DW8250 and ARM SMC support and add the XTX XT25F128
flash device. Patches 6-15 add the RK3588 SoC and its devices. Patches
16-18 add the ROCK 5B board and firmware boot support. Patch 19 adds
functional boot tests, and patch 20 adds documentation.

Both boot paths have been tested with Linux 6.12.4, with U-Boot 2025.01
used for the firmware boot path. The included functional tests cover
booting to a Buildroot shell, reboot, and poweroff.

Comments and suggestions are welcome.

Thanks,
Yifan

[PATCH v2] KVM: nSVM: Check full nested state size before writing to user buffer
Author: Priyanka Mani <priyankamani2100@gmail.com>

svm_get_nested_state() compares the userspace-provided buffer size against
kvm_state.size before adding KVM_STATE_NESTED_SVM_VMCB_SIZE for a vCPU that
is in guest mode.  When L2 is active and userspace passes a size that is
large enough for the header but too small for the vmcb12 payload, KVM thus
skips the early exit, copies out the header, clears and fills the full 4KiB
vmcb12 area past the end of the buffer described by userspace, and only
then does the caller notice that the returned size is larger than the
provided one and fail with -E2BIG.

As a result, KVM writes up to 4KiB beyond the buffer userspace said it
has, and if that memory isn't mapped, the ioctl fails with -EFAULT instead
of the documented -E2BIG without reporting the required size.

Move the size check to immediately before the first copy_to_user(), after
the header, including kvm_state.size, has been fully filled in, so that a
too-small buffer is rejected without writing anything to it, and so that
any future additions to the size are automatically covered by the check.

Fixes: cc440cdad5b7 ("KVM: nSVM: implement KVM_GET_NESTED_STATE and KVM_SET_NESTED_STATE")
Cc: stable@vger.kernel.org
Suggested-by: Dan Carpenter <dan@pereq.eu>
Signed-off-by: Priyanka Mani <priyankamani2100@gmail.com>
---

Notes:
    v2:
     - Move the user_data_size check to right before the first copy_to_user()
       instead of computing the size up front, so that future additions to
       kvm_state.size are covered automatically. [Dan, Sean]
     - Add Suggested-by: Dan Carpenter.
    
    v1: https://lore.kernel.org/all/57324d544da1e92cf3b30ff7c40595dfbca1933d.1794465097.git.priyankamani2100@gmail.com
    
    Tested on an AMD EPYC 7763 host by booting the patched kernel under QEMU
    (nested SVM) with KASAN and lockdep.  A reproducer enters L2, exits to
    userspace from L2 and calls KVM_GET_NESTED_STATE with
    size = sizeof(struct kvm_nested_state).  Without this patch, KVM writes
    4096 bytes past the 128-byte buffer before returning -E2BIG, or returns
    -EFAULT if the next page is unmapped.  With this patch, KVM returns
    -E2BIG and reports size = 4224 without writing to the buffer.
    
    KVM selftests nested_set_state_test, state_test, smm_test, hyperv_svm_test,
    svm_nested_vmcb12_gpa, svm_nested_pat_test and the other nested SVM tests
    give identical results with and without the patch.

 arch/x86/kvm/svm/nested.c | 6 +++---
 1 file changed, 3 insertions(+), 3 deletions(-)

[RFC PATCH 05/13] KVM: selftests: Cover plane run-state ioctls in planes_test
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


Assert that a new plane-1 vCPU starts with KVM_PLANE_RUN_STATE_INACTIVE
and plane 0's with no flags, that every valid combination of
KVM_PLANE_RUN_STATE_USER_HOLD, KVM_PLANE_RUN_STATE_SUSPENDED and
KVM_PLANE_RUN_STATE_INACTIVE reads back as written (so a saved state
restores), and that clearing them reads back as runnable. INACTIVE on
plane 0, INACTIVE together with SUSPENDED and the lowest undefined bit
are refused with EINVAL, and a refused SET leaves an existing hold in
place. On a VM that did not enable more than one plane both ioctls fail
with EINVAL.

With plane 0 held and nothing runnable, KVM_RUN still honours
immediate_exit with EINTR, and otherwise exits with KVM_EXIT_PLANE_EVENT
and cause KVM_PLANE_EVENT_HELD, with kvm_run's if_flag and apic_base
filled in as on any other exit. That exit leaves registers marked in
kvm_dirty_regs unconsumed and unrefreshed, and once plane 1 is released
they land in plane 1, not plane 0. If plane 0 holds a pending I/O
completion, here from a real-mode OUT, a held KVM_RUN fails with EBUSY,
with or without immediate_exit, leaves the KVM_EXIT_IO in kvm_run and
plane 0's RIP on the OUT, and the OUT retires once plane 0 is released.

Signed-off-by: David Woodhouse <dwmw@amazon.co.uk>
---
 tools/testing/selftests/kvm/x86/planes_test.c | 198 +++++++++++++++++-
 1 file changed, 196 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

[RFC PATCH 10/13] KVM: planes: Let plane twins share XCR0
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


XCR0/XFEM is shared between VTLs (TLFS 15.4). The planes series keeps
arch.xcr0 per vCPU, so an XSETBV in one VTL is invisible in the other,
and VTL1 reads a stale XFEM: a feature VTL0 enabled (AVX, for the
Windows secure kernel) is not enabled in VTL1's XCR0. The register
contents themselves are a separate matter, carried by the code that
switches VTLs.

Add KVM_PLANE_SHARE_XCR0 to the KVM_CAP_PLANES share mask. With it set,
propagate the validated value to every plane twin in __kvm_set_xcr(),
covering both guest XSETBV and the KVM_SET_XCRS ioctl path, and let a
twin created later inherit plane 0's current value. The CPUID
dynamic-bits dirty marker lives in the shared common state, so the
existing single set covers the twins. Update the planes section of
api.rst and the kvm_cpuid_apply_runtime() comment, which still listed
XCR0 among the per-plane registers.

SEV-SNP VMPLs bank XCR0 per VMSA, and sharing it there would be wrong
in two ways: sev_es_sync_from_ghcb() feeds a VMPL's GHCB XCR0 to
__kvm_set_xcr(), so a less-privileged VMPL would overwrite its siblings'
values (and the MSR-protocol CPUID leaf 0xD sizes would then follow the
wrong VMPL); and a pre-launch KVM_SET_XCRS on one twin would change
plane 0's VMSA, and with it the launch measurement. An SNP VM cannot
set the share mask, since KVM_CAP_PLANES refuses its VM type, so its
VMPLs keep XCR0 of their own by construction.

The twins' fpstate sizing does not depend on this bit: the planes share
CPUID, so any twin's own XSETBV can enable a dynamic xfeature whether or
not XCR0 is shared.

Signed-off-by: David Woodhouse <dwmw@amazon.co.uk>
---

 arch/x86/include/uapi/asm/kvm.h |  3 +++
 arch/x86/kvm/cpuid.c            |  4 +++-
 arch/x86/kvm/x86.c              | 34 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++

 5 files changed, 49 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)

[RFC PATCH 06/13] KVM: planes: Fan out per-plane requests to twin vCPUs
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


A request raised on one plane's vCPU for a VM-wide reason (TLB flush
after a host-side zap, APIC page reload, obsolete-roots release) is
consumed by whichever twin runs first, so the other planes keep stale
translations. The plane-1 twin of a Windows VSM guest then runs on a
zapped root and the guest bugchecks.

Mark those three requests per-plane and make kvm_make_vcpu_request()
replicate any KVM_REQUEST_PER_PLANE request to every twin of the
target vCPU through vcpu_for_each_plane(). Mode and wait live in the
shared common and the kick targets the running twin's pCPU, so the
existing kick logic already reaches the twins.

The x86 kvm_exit and powerpc kvm_check_requests tracepoints logged
only the shared request word, so the three requests would vanish from
them. OR in the vCPU's plane requests instead; a separate field would
say which word a bit came from, but changes the tracepoint format for
little gain.

Signed-off-by: David Woodhouse <dwmw@amazon.co.uk>
---
 arch/powerpc/kvm/trace.h        |  3 ++-
 arch/x86/include/asm/kvm_host.h |  6 ++++--
 arch/x86/kvm/mmu/mmu.c          | 13 ++++++++-----
 arch/x86/kvm/trace.h            |  3 ++-

[RFC PATCH 00/13] KVM: planes: Userspace API
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


The VSM work¹ drives planes from userspace, which takes a little
more API than the planes series² itself provides: an explicit
userspace opt-in (enabling KVM_CAP_PLANES with the plane count,
before any vCPU is created), KVM_GET/SET_PLANE_RUN_STATE vCPU
ioctls, and the mechanics to go with them — per-plane requests
fanned out to the twin vCPUs, XCR0 shared across twins (with every
twin's fpstate sized for the dynamic xfeatures that allows), and
APICv inhibited while planes are enabled (for now). With selftests,
including Paolo's plane infrastructure tests from the original
planes RFC³, ported forward.

Posted separately from the VSM RFC v2 (to follow) so this part can
be reviewed — or folded — with the rest of the planes series. Based
on that series² with my fixes⁴ applied (including two follow-up
patches on that thread); the whole stack is at ⁵.

¹ https://lore.kernel.org/kvm/20261017122501.610248-1-dwmw2@infradead.org/
² https://lore.kernel.org/kvm/20260608144252.351443-1-joro@8bytes.org/
³ https://lore.kernel.org/all/20250401161106.790710-29-pbonzini@redhat.com/
⁴ https://lore.kernel.org/kvm/20261111222911.477-1-dwmw2@infradead.org/
⁵ https://git.infradead.org/?p=users/dwmw2/linux.git;a=shortlog;h=refs/heads/vsm2/planes-uapi

[RFC PATCH 13/13] KVM: selftests: Cover plane state sharing
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


Check the KVM_CAP_PLANES share mask: KVM_CAP_PLANES_SHARE reports
XCR0|DR6 on the system fd, 0 on a VM fd before the opt-in and the
enabled bits after it; an unknown bit and a non-zero args[2] fail with
EINVAL, and a second opt-in with another mask fails with EEXIST.

Then, with no sharing and with XCR0 and DR6 shared, write XCR0 and DR6
through plane 0 before creating a plane-1 twin, and check that the twin
inherits them only when shared; write them through plane 1 and check
plane 0; and have plane 1 write DR6 with MOV (real mode, plane 0 held)
and check that plane 0 sees the guest's value only when DR6 is shared.

[RFC PATCH 08/13] KVM: planes: Enlarge every twin's fpstate for dynamic xfeatures
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


Plane twins share CPUID, so a twin's guest can set the XCR0 bits for a
dynamic xfeature such as AMX's XTILEDATA with its own XSETBV. But
kvm_check_cpuid() calls fpu_enable_guest_xfd_features() only for the
vCPU that KVM_SET_CPUID was issued on, and a twin created afterwards
starts with the default fpstate. A twin that then runs AMX code loses
its tile data on every fpstate save, because the fpstate it saves into
has no room for it. On a host that exposes AMX to the guest, any twin
using AMX hits this.

Whether the XRSTORS on the next entry can also leave a previous
context's tile registers in place for that twin to read is an open
question; it has not been verified.

Enlarge the fpstate of every twin in kvm_check_cpuid(), and give a twin
created after KVM_SET_CPUID the dynamic xfeatures of its plane-0
sibling's fpstate.

Only a twin that has not run may have its fpstate enlarged:
fpstate_realloc() hands a guest fpstate that is not loaded a fresh
image and copies nothing, so a twin that has run would lose its XSAVE
state. kvm_set_cpuid() already accepts only identical CPUID once the
vCPU has run, but it checked only the vCPU the ioctl was issued on.
With plane 0 held while a higher plane runs, KVM_SET_CPUID2 on plane 0
would pass that check and resize the plane that ran. Since the twins
share CPUID, take the identical-CPUID path once any twin has run.

Signed-off-by: David Woodhouse <dwmw@amazon.co.uk>
---
 arch/x86/kvm/cpuid.c | 21 +++++++++++++++++++--
 arch/x86/kvm/x86.c   | 17 +++++++++++++++++
 2 files changed, 36 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

[RFC PATCH 01/13] KVM: x86: Let userspace opt in to multiple planes with KVM_CAP_PLANES
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


A VMM implementing a software privilege model such as Hyper-V VSM
needs more than one plane, but kvm_arch_max_planes() returns the
vendor ceiling, which is 1 everywhere except SEV-SNP (where VMPLs are
guest-architectural), so KVM_CREATE_PLANE rejects every id above zero.

Make KVM_CAP_PLANES a VM enable capability taking the number of planes
userspace is going to manage, up to KVM_MAX_USER_PLANES (4), which the
system fd now reports. It must be set before any vCPU or extra plane
exists, so nothing is sized against the old limit, and cannot be changed
once set. It also needs a split irqchip, which kvm_arch_max_planes()
already requires before it reports more than one plane, so enabling it
without one fails instead of silently granting a single plane, and
KVM_CREATE_IRQCHIP is refused afterwards. Non-default VM types are
rejected: SEV-SNP keeps deriving its count from the supported VMPLs,
and protected-state VM types need their own audit first. So is more
than one plane without TDP: with shadow paging every plane would
write-track the guest page tables, and the per-gfn write-track count
is only 16 bits wide. The same count is why the opt-in stops at four
planes even with TDP: nested guests still have write-tracked shadow
pages, each plane can add its own for a gfn, and four planes is the
most that keeps the count below 2^16. KVM_MAX_PLANES stays 16, the
bound for plane ids. A VM that does not enable the capability keeps
reporting a single plane.

Signed-off-by: David Woodhouse <dwmw@amazon.co.uk>
---
 Documentation/virt/kvm/api.rst  | 36 +++++++++++++++++++++
 arch/x86/include/asm/kvm_host.h |  1 +
 arch/x86/kvm/x86.c              | 55 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++--
 include/linux/kvm_host.h        | 12 +++++++
 virt/kvm/kvm_main.c             | 11 ++++---
 5 files changed, 108 insertions(+), 7 deletions(-)

[RFC PATCH 09/13] KVM: x86: Let userspace choose state for plane twins to share
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


Which per-vCPU state the planes of a vCPU share is policy for the VMM.
Hyper-V VSM shares XCR0 between VTLs, and whether it shares DR6 depends
on the processor, as HvRegisterVsmCapabilities.Dr6Shared reports; a VMM
with a different privilege model may want neither. So far the only knob
is the plane count, which leaves nothing to key such sharing on but the
opt-in itself.

Take a mask of state to share in args[1] of KVM_ENABLE_CAP(KVM_CAP_PLANES),
set once together with the count. Unknown bits, and a non-zero args[2]
or args[3], fail with -EINVAL. A new KVM_CAP_PLANES_SHARE reports the
supported bits on the system fd and the enabled bits on a VM fd, as
KVM_CAP_PLANES reports the ceiling and the live count. No bits are
defined yet; the following patches add XCR0 and DR6.

Signed-off-by: David Woodhouse <dwmw@amazon.co.uk>
---

[RFC PATCH 04/13] KVM: Add KVM_GET/SET_PLANE_RUN_STATE vCPU ioctls
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


A VMM doing VSM in userspace has to switch the executing plane when
the guest asks for a privilege transition, but a vCPU's plane run
state is kernel-internal, set only by the SEV-SNP VMPL switch,
plane-0 event delivery, and vCPU creation. A VTL call forwarded to
userspace is a dead end.

Add ioctls that read and write a plane vCPU's run state as three flags.
KVM_PLANE_RUN_STATE_USER_HOLD is userspace policy: the plane is
stopped, and only KVM_SET_PLANE_RUN_STATE clears it again.
KVM_PLANE_RUN_STATE_SUSPENDED marks a plane suspended by a guest
privilege transition; KVM does not set it yet, but userspace can
restore it. KVM_PLANE_RUN_STATE_INACTIVE marks a non-zero plane that
has not been started, which is how every such plane begins; SUSPENDED
implies a started plane, so the two together are refused. A plane with
no flag is runnable. KVM_RUN already executes the lowest-numbered
runnable plane, so a userspace plane switch is just: clear the
target's flags, hold the current plane, re-enter KVM_RUN.

A held or suspended plane 0 *stays* stopped. Forcing it runnable on a
pending event fits the SEV-SNP model, but under a userspace privilege
model plane 0 would preempt the higher VTL on any interrupt and resume
mid-hypercall with whatever completion value the shared kvm_run last
held; the events stay pending until the VMM switches back. For the same
reason KVM_RUN no longer falls back to a held plane 0 when nothing is
runnable: it exits with KVM_EXIT_PLANE_EVENT and the new
KVM_PLANE_EVENT_HELD cause, through plane 0's normal exit path, so
immediate_exit and the kvm_run state userspace reads behave as for any
other exit. When KVM makes a plane runnable it clears the two
kernel-owned flags, SUSPENDED and INACTIVE, and never the hold.

The held exit must not disturb what the shared kvm_run carries for a
plane. It neither applies kvm_dirty_regs nor refreshes the
kvm_valid_regs area; those belong to the plane that runs next. A
completion still pending from plane 0's last exit is harder. Running it
first, as any other KVM_RUN would, is right only if kvm_run still holds
that exit's data, and KVM cannot tell whether it does: a VMM doing VSM
holds plane 0 inside a forwarded VtlCall while VTL1 runs, VTL1's exits
reuse the same union, and the completion would retire the VtlCall with
VTL1's bytes. Exiting with the completion still armed would instead
overwrite completion data the VMM did write. So with a completion
pending, a held KVM_RUN fails with -EBUSY and leaves kvm_run alone; the
completion runs when plane 0 next runs.

The flags round-trip: a VMM that saves them and writes them back, for
example on a migration destination, restores the same state, including
a plane that was never started. Which runnable plane happens to be
executing is not part of the interface.

The ioctls are for planes userspace manages, so they return -EINVAL
unless the VM enabled more than one plane with KVM_CAP_PLANES. That
leaves SEV-SNP VMPL planes, whose count and switching the architecture
defines, outside the interface, and it keeps other architectures out:
only x86 consumes KVM_REQ_PLANE_RESCHED, and elsewhere a stray request
bit would abort every subsequent entry attempt, leaving KVM_RUN
spinning at 100% with no way for the VMM to recover the vCPU.

Signed-off-by: David Woodhouse <dwmw@amazon.co.uk>
---
 Documentation/virt/kvm/api.rst |  69 +++++++++++++++++++++
 arch/x86/kvm/x86.c             |  41 ++++++++++---
 include/linux/kvm_host.h       |  21 ++++++-
 include/uapi/linux/kvm.h       |   6 ++

[RFC PATCH 11/13] KVM: planes: Let plane twins share DR6
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


The TLFS leaves DR6 sharing between VTLs processor-dependent and tells
the guest which way it went in HvRegisterVsmCapabilities.Dr6Shared. KVM
cannot know what the VMM advertises there, so make it a share bit and
let the VMM keep the two consistent: KVM_PLANE_SHARE_DR6.

The twins keep their own arch.dr6, kept equal whenever no plane is in
the guest:

 - after each plane's run in the KVM_RUN plane loop, its DR6 is copied
   to the twins. By then the exit has synced any DR6 the guest owned in
   hardware back into arch.dr6 (sync_dirty_debug_regs() on VMX and SVM,
   under KVM_DEBUGREG_WONT_EXIT); otherwise DR accesses are intercepted
   and arch.dr6 is the only copy. A VTL switch done in the kernel
   returns through the same loop;
 - KVM_SET_DEBUGREGS copies the new value to the twins at once;
 - a twin created later starts with plane 0's DR6, as for XCR0.

Converting every arch.dr6 user to a shared location instead would touch
the vendor, nested, SMM and exception code for no gain. Writers audited
for the copy-after-run rule:

 - MOV to DR6 (emulated, or direct under KVM_DEBUGREG_WONT_EXIT), #DB
   payload delivery (kvm_deliver_exception_payload()), RSM, INIT and
   nested VMRUN on SVM all write the DR6 of the plane that is running,
   so the next copy carries them;
 - a #DB still pending on a plane that is switched away from updates
   DR6 when that plane next takes it, which matches a debug exception
   that has not been delivered yet;
 - host-side debugging (KVM_SET_GUEST_DEBUG) reports in
   kvm_run->debug.arch.dr6 and leaves arch.dr6 alone;
 - vCPU ioctls on twins take the shared vCPU mutex, so none of them can
   observe a plane mid-run.

DR0-DR3 and DR7 stay per plane.

Signed-off-by: David Woodhouse <dwmw@amazon.co.uk>
---
 Documentation/virt/kvm/api.rst  |  5 +++++
 arch/x86/include/uapi/asm/kvm.h |  1 +
 arch/x86/kvm/regs.c             |  1 +
 arch/x86/kvm/x86.c              | 25 +++++++++++++++++++++++++
 arch/x86/kvm/x86.h              |  4 +++-
 5 files changed, 35 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

[RFC PATCH 07/13] KVM: x86: Inhibit APICv when VM planes are enabled
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


With APICv live on plane-0 vCPUs while their plane-1 twins use
software injection, the twins trip the APICv coherency checks: stale
vcpu-vs-VM activation state at vcpu_enter_guest(), and timer interrupt
injection against a vCPU that is not the running one. Cross-plane
event delivery depends on software-injection bookkeeping that hardware
acceleration bypasses.

Add APICV_INHIBIT_REASON_PLANES, set it when a plane above 0 is
created, and include it in the VMX and AVIC required-inhibit masks;
__kvm_set_or_clear_apicv_inhibit() silently ignores a reason the
vendor mask does not carry. Drop the inhibit again when the
KVM_CREATE_PLANE error path destroys the last nonzero plane of a live
VM; at VM teardown plane 0 is already gone and the update is skipped.

Signed-off-by: David Woodhouse <dwmw@amazon.co.uk>
---

 arch/x86/kvm/vmx/main.c |  3 ++-

 arch/x86/kvm/x86.h      |  8 ++++++++

[RFC PATCH 12/13] KVM: selftests: Check CPUID handling of plane twins
Author: David Woodhouse <dwmw2@infradead.org>


Two checks of how plane twins follow the CPUID they share.

A plane vCPU created after KVM_SET_CPUID gets its Hyper-V context in
kvm_arch_vcpu_create(), before its vCPU index is assigned, and used to
latch a VP index of 0xFFFFFFFF. Create two vCPUs, give them a Hyper-V
CPUID, then create their plane-1 twins and check that
HV_X64_MSR_VP_INDEX on each twin reads its vCPU index.

Once a twin has run, KVM_SET_CPUID2 must accept only the CPUID already
set, even on a plane 0 that has never run. Run plane 1 with plane 0
held (a real-mode OUT, then retired), and check that identical CPUID is
accepted and that CPUID with XTILE added to leaf 0xD fails with EINVAL.
Before kvm_set_cpuid() checked every twin, that call went on to the
fpstate code, which failed it with EPERM, or resized the twin that ran
if the process had AMX permission.

Signed-off-by: David Woodhouse <dwmw@amazon.co.uk>

[PATCH v3] selftests/cgroup: Make the object directory an order-only prerequisite
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

The cgroup_util.o rule in libcgroup.mk lists the object directory as a
normal prerequisite. The KVM selftests include libcgroup.mk, so for
them that directory is their own $(OUTPUT)/lib. After any incremental
KVM build that rebuilds an object there, the directory is newer than
cgroup_util.o, and the next build recompiles cgroup_util.o and relinks
every test.

Make the directory an order-only prerequisite, as libvfio.mk does.

Acked-by: Michal Koutný <mkoutny@suse.com>
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---

    This was patch 2/2 of v2 [1]. Sean took patch 1 through kvm-x86 and
    asked for this one to go through the cgroup tree [2].
    
    Changes since v2:
    - Collected Michal's Acked-by.
    - Rebased on cgroup/for-7.5.
    - Dropped the Fixes: tag, as the patch only saves a rebuild.

[kvm-unit-tests PATCH] Move position-independant code flags into the target specific Makefiles
Author: Thomas Huth <thuth@redhat.com>

The settings for position-independant code are currently quite a mess:
It depends on the target whether we need the flags or rather should even
disable PIC / PIE, but currently the settings are spread between the
main Makefile (-fno-pic is the default, unless CONFIG_EFI has been set)
and the target specific Makefiles.
This leads to some weird situations where we have -fno-pic in the CFLAGS,
but link with -pie in the LDFLAGS since we still want to get a relocation
table, but then again need to add a "-z notext" to the linker flags to
avoid warnings about relocation entries in the text segment.
Let's face it: Always adding -fno-pic / -no-pie in the main Makefile is
a bad idea. Move the settings to the target specific Makefiles instead
where we know what we really want, and then we can finally drop the
"-z notext" settings, too.

Signed-off-by: Thomas Huth <thuth@redhat.com>

[PATCH v2] KVM: arm64: Register irq_bypass consumers only when direct MSIs are possible
Author: Jinqian Yang <yangjinqian1@huawei.com>

kvm_arch_has_irq_bypass() on arm64 returns true unconditionally, so
every irqfd assignment registers an irq_bypass consumer even when KVM
cannot do direct MSI forwarding for the VM. In that case
kvm_vgic_v4_set_forwarding() bails out at its very first check, since
vgic_supports_direct_msis() is false, and the registration becomes pure
overhead: every KVM_IRQFD ioctl takes the global irq_bypass mutex, which
becomes a contention point when many VMs boot concurrently.

Pass struct kvm to kvm_arch_has_irq_bypass() and gate the registration
on vgic_supports_direct_msis(kvm), like x86 gates on
enable_device_posted_irqs. Reading the per-VM vgic state is safe here,
as kvm_irqfd_assign() requires an initialized irqchip.

Functionally this is a no-op: without direct MSI support the consumer
registration never did anything beyond acquiring the lock. On a
GICv3-only host booting 100 microVMs concurrently this cuts the average
boot time by ~5 ms, matching the gain observed with
CONFIG_HAVE_KVM_IRQ_BYPASS=n.

Signed-off-by: Jinqian Yang <yangjinqian1@huawei.com>
---
v1 -> v2:
 - Gate on vgic_supports_direct_msis(kvm) instead of the global
   kvm_vgic_global_state.has_gicv4 flag, This requires passing struct
   kvm to kvm_arch_has_irq_bypass() and updating the x86 and PPC callers.

v1: https://lore.kernel.org/linux-arm-kernel/20261109112018.241298-1-yangjinqian1@huawei.com/

[PATCH] KVM: arm64: Keep firmware Spectre-v4 mitigation on for protected guests
Author: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>

EL2 handles a protected guest's ARM_SMCCC_ARCH_WORKAROUND_2 discovery
query from the host's Spectre-v4 state at init. If the system is
mitigated and the guest doesn't have SSBS, it returns NOT_REQUIRED,
which means that firmware mitigation is permanently enabled. However,
the host can disable the mitigation before running a protected vCPU,
with an ARM_SMCCC_ARCH_WORKAROUND_2 call that EL2 forwards to the
firmware.

The host makes that call for its own userspace, so keep forwarding it,
without hints so that the firmware decodes it as EL2 does. Track it per
CPU, and on a mitigated system enable the mitigation again before
running a protected vCPU on a CPU where the host has disabled it.
Firmware enables it when a CPU powers up, so the tracked state isn't
reset on CPU_ON or resume.

Fixes: fe40ff87ead5c ("KVM: arm64: Implement HVC handling for protected guests at EL2")
Reported-by: Ryan Roberts <ryan.roberts@arm.com>
Closes: https://lore.kernel.org/all/5fa8a1fb-4e2c-4746-8a70-a47f387ef0d6@arm.com/
Signed-off-by: Fuad Tabba <fuad.tabba@linux.dev>
---

Notes:
    Tested on QEMU with TF-A. Upstream TF-A implements the dynamic
    workaround only for Cortex-A76, so I patched it to advertise the
    workaround on the Cortex-A57 that QEMU models. With a host that
    disables the mitigation before running a protected vCPU, TF-A gets the
    call to enable it again from EL2 with this patch, and doesn't without
    it. TF-A's handler for the call does nothing there and QEMU doesn't
    model the mitigation, so this tests the calls and not their effect.
    
    Calling the firmware before every protected vCPU run would do without
    the tracking, at the cost of an SMC per run.
    
    Based on Linux 7.4-rc2 (7475ea8b58616).

[PATCH v3] KVM: arm64: Resume stage-2 range walks where they stopped
Author: Haseeb Ashraf <haseebashraf091@gmail.com>


stage2_apply_range() walks a range one block-sized chunk at a time.
The walker steps over an invalid entry in one go, but the next walk
starts at the following chunk, so an invalid entry larger than a chunk
is visited once for every chunk it covers. Unmapping the whole IPA
space of a shadow stage-2, as emulating TLBI VMALLS12E1IS and TLBI
ALLE1IS does, takes 2^18 walks with 4K pages and a 48-bit IPA space,
however little it maps. Each walk also ends with a range TLBI when
FEAT_TLBIRANGE and FWB are available.

On an AmpereOne A192-32X (192 CPUs, Xen on KVM, 4K pages, 48-bit IPA),
a full-range unmap of one shadow stage-2 took 0.84-0.94 s (mean 0.91 s
over 226 unmaps) on 6.18.29. With this change and the mainline commits
it builds on backported, it took 2.1-45 ms (mean 4.1 ms over 672
unmaps), with 512 to 1023 walks instead of 262144. The slowest TLBI
VMALLS12E1IS trap took 0.94 s before and 46 ms after.

Have the walk report the address it reached, and have
stage2_apply_range() resume from there. Unmap, write-protect and flush
all go through it, so all three get this. kvm_pgtable_stage2_unmap(),
kvm_pgtable_stage2_wrprotect() and kvm_pgtable_stage2_flush() now
return the address, or a negative error code, and so do their pKVM
counterparts, which do not walk page tables and return the end of the
range. The other callers, in nested.c and at EL2, only looked for an
error: they now check for a negative value, and those that pass the
result on turn it back into 0. The walk handles every entry it steps
over, including invalid entries that carry state, so nothing has to be
looked up beforehand.

The unmap of a shadow stage-2 also drops the guest stage-2 records of
the whole span the walk covered: nothing is mapped in the part it
stepped over, so any record there is stale. Each walk that remains
ends with the same deferred TLB invalidation as before. The spans that
were skipped have no valid entry, so there is nothing to invalidate.

Suggested-by: Oliver Upton <oupton@kernel.org>
Signed-off-by: Haseeb Ashraf <haseeb.ashraf@siemens.com>
---
